Not really like… voices, I don’t think. It’s more that I know something is going on at work. The supervisors keep changing my shifts, and everyone says, “Oh, it’s just staffing shortages,” but that doesn’t feel right. The schedule changes, the way they look at me after, it all connects. I try to explain it and then they just say I’m stressed, but I’m pretty sure they’re monitoring me.

I don’t think it’s like… somebody putting thoughts in there, no. Or taking them out. I don’t really know what that would feel like.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really like someone is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out. I keep saying that part isn’t… it’s not exactly it.

It’s more that I know they’re watching me. Like the supervisors, through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get changed after. People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, like that explains everything, but it doesn’t. They move me around and watch how I act, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe or something.

And then if I try to explain that, it sounds like I’m not answering right, but that’s the thing I mean. It’s not my thoughts being pulled out, it’s them already checking on me and changing things because of me.

Yeah… yeah, that’s more like what I mean. Like, at work, when the supervisors move my shifts around, it’s not random to me. They say it’s staffing shortages, everybody keeps saying that, but it feels too lined up with what I’m doing.

Like the cameras on the unit are there, and then I’ll notice someone watching me, or acting different, and then suddenly my schedule changes or I’m put somewhere else. It feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe. Other people don’t seem to see that connection, but I do.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not like songs or TV so much… I don’t really watch TV the same way lately anyway, it’s usually just on in the room and people are talking over it.

It’s more at work. Like I said, the supervisors… they watch through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get moved around. People keep saying, “Oh, it’s staffing,” but it’s not just that. So I don’t know if that counts as secret messages. It’s not a song telling me something. It’s more like the things they do are about me, and everybody acts like I’m not supposed to notice.

It’s not my thoughts being pulled out, it’s them already checking on me and changing things because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that are happening around you, like things people say or do, or events in general, are directly related to you in a special way that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s more like what I mean. Like, at work, when the supervisors move my shifts around, it’s not random to me. They say it’s staffing shortages, everybody keeps saying that, but it feels too lined up with what I’m doing.\n\nLike the cameras on the unit are there, and then I’ll notice someone watching me, or acting different, and then suddenly my schedule changes or I’m put somewhere else. It feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe. Other people don’t seem to see that connection, but I do.\n\nAnd I know it sounds like I’m making a big thing out of scheduling, but it’s not just scheduling. It’s the way it happens after they’ve been watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random things people do or say—like something on TV, a song playing, or even strangers talking—are sending you secret messages meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Not like songs or TV so much… I don’t really watch TV the same way lately anyway, it’s usually just on in the room and people are talking over it.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like I said, the supervisors… they watch through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get moved around. People keep saying, “Oh, it’s staffing,” but it’s not just that. It happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong.\n\nSo I don’t know if that counts as secret messages. It’s not a song telling me something. It’s more like the things they do are about me, and everybody acts like I’m not supposed to notice.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever feel like your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of yourself, like someone or something is influencing them?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / orderly_or_psychiatric_aide / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m sure the supervisors changed my shifts and watch me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe, even when people tell me it’s just staffing shortages.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if controlled is the right word. Not like a remote control or something. It’s more like… when I’m at work, I start thinking about how I’m acting because I know they’re watching through the unit cameras.

And then the supervisors change my shifts, and everybody says, “It’s just staffing shortages,” but it doesn’t feel like just staffing. It’s always after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe.

So maybe that influences my thoughts, yeah, because I can’t stop thinking about what they’re seeing or what they’re going to do with it. But I wouldn’t say they’re putting thoughts in there. It’s just… they’re watching, and then things change because of me.

It’s more like… when I’m at work, I start thinking about how I’m acting because I know they’re watching through the unit cameras.\n\nAnd then the supervisors change my shifts, and everybody says, “It’s just staffing shortages,” but it doesn’t feel like just staffing. It’s always after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe.\n\nSo maybe that influences my thoughts, yeah, because I can’t stop thinking about what they’re seeing or what they’re going to do with it. But I wouldn’t say they’re putting thoughts in there. It’s just… they’re watching, and then things change because of me.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever feel like the thoughts you’re having are being placed in your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being heard or known by other people around you without you saying them out loud?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think they’re, like, putting thoughts in my head exactly. Or hearing them word-for-word. It’s more… they already know what they’re looking for with me.

Like at work, with the cameras on the unit, I’m sure the supervisors are watching me to see if I’m unsafe. Then my shifts get changed, and everyone keeps saying it’s staffing shortages, but that doesn’t make sense to me because it happens after they’ve been watching. So maybe they don’t hear my thoughts, but they know enough. They act like they know what I’m going to do before I do it.

And then I start thinking about it more, and I probably look nervous, and then that probably just makes them watch more. It’s hard to explain.

S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: I barely get hungry anymore, so a lot of days I just drink coffee at work and maybe eat vending-machine crackers, and my clothes have gotten loose.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: People tell me I move like I’m underwater, and then other times I can’t sit still and keep pacing my apartment or rubbing my hands until they’re sore.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: I can read the same page of a patient note or a bill three times and still not know what it says, and even picking what to buy at the store can freeze me up.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: After bad nights I keep thinking it would be easier if I didn’t wake up, and sometimes the thought comes that I should just end it, even though I get scared and tell someone.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When the depression is at its worst, I’m also most convinced the supervisors are watching me through the cameras, and my speech gets so tangled that I can’t explain myself clearly.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: Looking back, the heavy empty mood and loss of interest have been there for most of this whole period, even during times when the camera worries or scattered talking were quieter.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a stretch of a few weeks when I wasn’t in that deep depressed place, but I still believed the cameras and supervisors were monitoring me and I kept acting guarded at work.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I’ve needed coworkers to double-check my rounds and charting, and at home I’ve fallen behind on bills, ignored friends, and let my apartment get out of control. It got bad enough that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I admitted I was thinking about suicide.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, starting after my job switched from steady day shifts to rotating nights and overtime.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 1 year and 1 month\n  manifestation: The beliefs about being watched and the disorganized behavior have been happening for about 1 year and 1 month.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The depression and loss of interest have been with me for about 1 year and 2 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / orderly_or_psychiatric_aide / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S021\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random things people do or say—like something on TV, a song playing, or even strangers talking—are sending you secret messages meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Not like songs or TV so much… I don’t really watch TV the same way lately anyway, it’s usually just on in the room and people are talking over it.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like I said, the supervisors… they watch through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get moved around. People keep saying, “Oh, it’s staffing,” but it’s not just that. It happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong.\n\nSo I don’t know if that counts as secret messages. It’s not a song telling me something. It’s more like the things they do are about me, and everybody acts like I’m not supposed to notice.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of yourself, like someone or something is influencing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if controlled is the right word. Not like a remote control or something. It’s more like… when I’m at work, I start thinking about how I’m acting because I know they’re watching through the unit cameras.\n\nAnd then the supervisors change my shifts, and everybody says, “It’s just staffing shortages,” but it doesn’t feel like just staffing. It’s always after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe.\n\nSo maybe that influences my thoughts, yeah, because I can’t stop thinking about what they’re seeing or what they’re going to do with it. But I wouldn’t say they’re putting thoughts in there. It’s just… they’re watching, and then things change because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like the thoughts you’re having are being placed in your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being heard or known by other people around you without you saying them out loud?\n\nPatient: I don’t think they’re, like, putting thoughts in my head exactly. Or hearing them word-for-word. It’s more… they already know what they’re looking for with me.\n\nLike at work, with the cameras on the unit, I’m sure the supervisors are watching me to see if I’m unsafe. Then my shifts get changed, and everyone keeps saying it’s staffing shortages, but that doesn’t make sense to me because it happens after they’ve been watching. So maybe they don’t hear my thoughts, but they know enough. They act like they know what I’m going to do before I do it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about it more, and I probably look nervous, and then that probably just makes them watch more. It’s hard to explain.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever feel like your thoughts are being placed in your mind by someone else, or like they're being removed from your mind, or that other people can hear your thoughts?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… not exactly like somebody is taking my thoughts out or putting them in, no. I don’t know how that would even feel.

But people act like they already know something about me. Like at work, the supervisors watch through the unit cameras, and then suddenly my shifts get changed. Everybody keeps saying it’s just staffing shortages, like I’m supposed to just accept that, but it keeps happening after they’ve been watching me. So it feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.

It’s not that they can hear every thought in my head. It’s more like they know enough from watching, and then they do things because of it. And then I start thinking about that all day, even when I’m trying to just do my job.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23.0, F23.1, F23.2, F23.3, F23.8, F23.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came because… I can’t keep my words in a straight line anymore. Like I’ll start trying to explain something from an overnight shift, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, or paint colors, or patient charts, and people just kind of stare at me like they’re lost. I can see it happening, but I can’t seem to stop the jumping around.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any unusual thoughts or perceptions, like hearing voices or seeing things that others don’t?\n\nPatient: Not really like… voices, I don’t think. It’s more that I know something is going on at work. The supervisors keep changing my shifts, and everyone says, “Oh, it’s just staffing shortages,” but that doesn’t feel right. It’s like they’re watching me through the unit cameras, checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nAnd I mean, cameras are there anyway, right? Like in the halls, near the nurses’ station, all that. So people act like I’m making a big leap, but I’m not. The schedule changes, the way they look at me after, it all connects. I try to explain it and then they just say I’m stressed, but I’m pretty sure they’re monitoring me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain events, actions, or even things people say are specifically directed at you or have a special meaning for you that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s kind of what I mean. Like with the shift changes, it’s not just random. People keep saying it’s staffing shortages, but I’m sure the supervisors changed my shifts on purpose, like they’re testing me or watching if I’m unsafe.\n\nAnd the cameras on the unit… everyone acts like, “Well, of course there are cameras,” but it’s the way they look at me after, you know? Like something happened on the footage, or they’re checking me. I’ll be trying to explain that, and then I think about how the night shift used to be easier because the halls were quieter, except then they changed that too, and it all kind of points back to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being taken out of your head or broadcasted to others?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being put in or taken out exactly… it’s not like I can point to that. It’s more like they already know things, like the supervisors are watching me through the unit cameras and then changing my shifts after, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe.\n\nPeople keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, but that doesn’t line up to me. Because the cameras are there, and then someone looks at me a certain way, and then my schedule gets moved, and then I’m on a different floor or different hours, and… sorry, I know that’s not exactly what you asked. But it feels connected. Like they’re monitoring me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being placed in your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being removed from your mind, or even that your thoughts are being heard by others around you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like… somebody putting thoughts in there, no. Or taking them out. I don’t really know what that would feel like.\n\nThe heard-by-others thing… I mean, sometimes I wonder if people know what I’m thinking, but then I don’t know if that’s just because I’m acting different and they can tell. Like if I’m quiet on the unit, people notice, and then they start looking at me, and then I start thinking about what I was doing before that, and it kind of… I lose the thread.\n\nSo I guess, no, not exactly like my thoughts are being broadcast. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being placed in your mind by someone else, or like they're being taken out of your head, or that other people can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Not really like someone is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out. I keep saying that part isn’t… it’s not exactly it.\n\nIt’s more that I know they’re watching me. Like the supervisors, through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get changed after. People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, like that explains everything, but it doesn’t. They move me around and watch how I act, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe or something.\n\nAnd then if I try to explain that, it sounds like I’m not answering right, but that’s the thing I mean. It’s not my thoughts being pulled out, it’s them already checking on me and changing things because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that are happening around you, like things people say or do, or events in general, are directly related to you in a special way that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s more like what I mean. Like, at work, when the supervisors move my shifts around, it’s not random to me. They say it’s staffing shortages, everybody keeps saying that, but it feels too lined up with what I’m doing.\n\nLike the cameras on the unit are there, and then I’ll notice someone watching me, or acting different, and then suddenly my schedule changes or I’m put somewhere else. It feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe. Other people don’t seem to see that connection, but I do.\n\nAnd I know it sounds like I’m making a big thing out of scheduling, but it’s not just scheduling. It’s the way it happens after they’ve been watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random things people do or say—like something on TV, a song playing, or even strangers talking—are sending you secret messages meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Not like songs or TV so much… I don’t really watch TV the same way lately anyway, it’s usually just on in the room and people are talking over it.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like I said, the supervisors… they watch through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get moved around. People keep saying, “Oh, it’s staffing,” but it’s not just that. It happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong.\n\nSo I don’t know if that counts as secret messages. It’s not a song telling me something. It’s more like the things they do are about me, and everybody acts like I’m not supposed to notice.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of yourself, like someone or something is influencing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if controlled is the right word. Not like a remote control or something. It’s more like… when I’m at work, I start thinking about how I’m acting because I know they’re watching through the unit cameras.\n\nAnd then the supervisors change my shifts, and everybody says, “It’s just staffing shortages,” but it doesn’t feel like just staffing. It’s always after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe.\n\nSo maybe that influences my thoughts, yeah, because I can’t stop thinking about what they’re seeing or what they’re going to do with it. But I wouldn’t say they’re putting thoughts in there. Or hearing them word-for-word. It feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe. Other people don’t seem to see that connection, but I do.\n\nAnd I know it sounds like I’m making a big thing out of scheduling, but it’s not just scheduling. It’s the way it happens after they’ve been watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random things people do or say—like something on TV, a song playing, or even strangers talking—are sending you secret messages meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Not like songs or TV so much… I don’t really watch TV the same way lately anyway, it’s usually just on in the room and people are talking over it.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like I said, the supervisors… they watch through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get moved around. People keep saying, “Oh, it’s staffing,” but it’s not just that. It happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong.\n\nSo I don’t know if that counts as secret messages. It’s not a song telling me something. It’s more like the things they do are about me, and everybody acts like I’m not supposed to notice.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of yourself, like someone or something is influencing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if controlled is the right word. Not like a remote control or something. It’s more like… when I’m at work, I start thinking about how I’m acting because I know they’re watching through the unit cameras.\n\nAnd then the supervisors change my shifts, and everybody says, “It’s just staffing shortages,” but it doesn’t feel like just staffing. It’s always after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe.\n\nSo maybe that influences my thoughts, yeah, because I can’t stop thinking about what they’re seeing or what they’re going to do with it. But I wouldn’t say they’re putting thoughts in there. It’s just… they’re watching, and then things change because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like the thoughts you’re having are being placed in your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being heard or known by other people around you without you saying them out loud?\n\nPatient: I don’t think they’re, like, putting thoughts in my head exactly. Or hearing them word-for-word. It’s more… they already know what they’re looking for with me.\n\nLike at work, with the cameras on the unit, I’m sure the supervisors are watching me to see if I’m unsafe. Then my shifts get changed, and everyone keeps saying it’s staffing shortages, but that doesn’t make sense to me because it happens after they’ve been watching. So maybe they don’t hear my thoughts, but they know enough. They act like they know what I’m going to do before I do it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about it more, and I probably look nervous, and then that probably just makes them watch more. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being placed in your mind by someone else, or like they're being removed from your mind, or that other people can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: I mean… not exactly like somebody is taking my thoughts out or putting them in, no. I don’t know how that would even feel.\n\nBut people act like they already know something about me. Like at work, the supervisors watch through the unit cameras, and then suddenly my shifts get changed. Everybody keeps saying it’s just staffing shortages, like I’m supposed to just accept that, but it keeps happening after they’ve been watching me. So it feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nIt’s not that they can hear every thought in my head. It’s more like they know enough from watching, and then they do things because of it. And then I start thinking about that all day, even when I’m trying to just do my job.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… yeah, that happens. Like with my shifts at work. People act like it’s just scheduling or staffing shortages, but I don’t buy that.

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… not like TV shows or, I don’t know, billboards or something. I don’t really mean it like that.

It’s more at work. Like the cameras on the unit — I’m sure the supervisors watch me through them, and then my shifts get changed. People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, but it always feels like it happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess something up.

And then everyone acts normal, like I’m supposed to not notice. I used to try to ignore it and just do my job, but once you see the pattern it’s hard to unsee it. Like even if they don’t say it directly, it feels like the whole thing is about me.

But it feels connected. Like they’re monitoring me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being placed in your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being removed from your mind, or even that your thoughts are being heard by others around you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like… somebody putting thoughts in there, no. Or taking them out. I don’t really know what that would feel like.\n\nThe heard-by-others thing… I mean, sometimes I wonder if people know what I’m thinking, but then I don’t know if that’s just because I’m acting different and they can tell. Like if I’m quiet on the unit, people notice, and then they start looking at me, and then I start thinking about what I was doing before that, and it kind of… I lose the thread.\n\nSo I guess, no, not exactly like my thoughts are being broadcast. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being placed in your mind by someone else, or like they're being taken out of your head, or that other people can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Not really like someone is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out. I keep saying that part isn’t… it’s not exactly it.\n\nIt’s more that I know they’re watching me. Like the supervisors, through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get changed after. People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, like that explains everything, but it doesn’t. They move me around and watch how I act, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe or something.\n\nAnd then if I try to explain that, it sounds like I’m not answering right, but that’s the thing I mean. It’s not my thoughts being pulled out, it’s them already checking on me and changing things because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that are happening around you, like things people say or do, or events in general, are directly related to you in a special way that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s more like what I mean. Like, at work, when the supervisors move my shifts around, it’s not random to me. They say it’s staffing shortages, everybody keeps saying that, but it feels too lined up with what I’m doing.\n\nLike the cameras on the unit are there, and then I’ll notice someone watching me, or acting different, and then suddenly my schedule changes or I’m put somewhere else. It feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe. Other people don’t seem to see that connection, but I do.\n\nAnd I know it sounds like I’m making a big thing out of scheduling, but it’s not just scheduling. It’s the way it happens after they’ve been watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random things people do or say—like something on TV, a song playing, or even strangers talking—are sending you secret messages meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Not like songs or TV so much… I don’t really watch TV the same way lately anyway, it’s usually just on in the room and people are talking over it.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like I said, the supervisors… they watch through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get moved around. People keep saying, “Oh, it’s staffing,” but it’s not just that. It happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong.\n\nSo I don’t know if that counts as secret messages. It’s not a song telling me something. It’s more like the things they do are about me, and everybody acts like I’m not supposed to notice.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of yourself, like someone or something is influencing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if controlled is the right word. Not like a remote control or something. It’s more like… when I’m at work, I start thinking about how I’m acting because I know they’re watching through the unit cameras.\n\nAnd then the supervisors change my shifts, and everybody says, “It’s just staffing shortages,” but it doesn’t feel like just staffing. It’s always after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe.\n\nSo maybe that influences my thoughts, yeah, because I can’t stop thinking about what they’re seeing or what they’re going to do with it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not like… someone is moving my arms or making words come out of my mouth. I don’t think it’s like that.

But at work, it kind of feels like they’re controlling what happens to me, if that makes sense. Like the supervisors watch me through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get changed. Everybody keeps saying it’s just staffing shortages, but that’s too easy. It’s always after they’ve been watching, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.

And then I start changing how I act because of it, like trying not to look nervous, trying to be careful where I stand, and then it makes me look worse probably. I don’t know. So not controlling my thoughts exactly, but they get in my head, because I know they’re watching.

Like they’re monitoring me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being placed in your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being removed from your mind, or even that your thoughts are being heard by others around you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like… somebody putting thoughts in there, no. Like, at work, when the supervisors move my shifts around, it’s not random to me. They say it’s staffing shortages, everybody keeps saying that, but it feels too lined up with what I’m doing.\n\nLike the cameras on the unit are there, and then I’ll notice someone watching me, or acting different, and then suddenly my schedule changes or I’m put somewhere else. It feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe. Other people don’t seem to see that connection, but I do.\n\nAnd I know it sounds like I’m making a big thing out of scheduling, but it’s not just scheduling. It’s the way it happens after they’ve been watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random things people do or say—like something on TV, a song playing, or even strangers talking—are sending you secret messages meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Not like songs or TV so much… I don’t really watch TV the same way lately anyway, it’s usually just on in the room and people are talking over it.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like I said, the supervisors… they watch through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get moved around. People keep saying, “Oh, it’s staffing,” but it’s not just that. It happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong.\n\nSo I don’t know if that counts as secret messages. It’s not a song telling me something. It’s more like the things they do are about me, and everybody acts like I’m not supposed to notice.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of yourself, like someone or something is influencing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if controlled is the right word. Not like a remote control or something. It’s more like… when I’m at work, I start thinking about how I’m acting because I know they’re watching through the unit cameras.\n\nAnd then the supervisors change my shifts, and everybody says, “It’s just staffing shortages,” but it doesn’t feel like just staffing. It’s not my thoughts being pulled out, it’s them already checking on me and changing things because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that are happening around you, like things people say or do, or events in general, are directly related to you in a special way that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s more like what I mean. Like, at work, when the supervisors move my shifts around, it’s not random to me. They say it’s staffing shortages, everybody keeps saying that, but it feels too lined up with what I’m doing.\n\nLike the cameras on the unit are there, and then I’ll notice someone watching me, or acting different, and then suddenly my schedule changes or I’m put somewhere else. It feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe. Other people don’t seem to see that connection, but I do.\n\nAnd I know it sounds like I’m making a big thing out of scheduling, but it’s not just scheduling. It’s the way it happens after they’ve been watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random things people do or say—like something on TV, a song playing, or even strangers talking—are sending you secret messages meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Not like songs or TV so much… I don’t really watch TV the same way lately anyway, it’s usually just on in the room and people are talking over it.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like I said, the supervisors… they watch through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get moved around. People keep saying, “Oh, it’s staffing,” but it’s not just that. It happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong.\n\nSo I don’t know if that counts as secret messages. It’s not a song telling me something. It’s more like the things they do are about me, and everybody acts like I’m not supposed to notice.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of yourself, like someone or something is influencing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if controlled is the right word. Not like a remote control or something. It’s more like… when I’m at work, I start thinking about how I’m acting because I know they’re watching through the unit cameras.\n\nAnd then the supervisors change my shifts, and everybody says, “It’s just staffing shortages,” but it doesn’t feel like just staffing. It’s always after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe.\n\nSo maybe that influences my thoughts, yeah, because I can’t stop thinking about what they’re seeing or what they’re going to do with it. But I wouldn’t say they’re putting thoughts in there. It’s just… they’re watching, and then things change because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like the thoughts you’re having are being placed in your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being heard or known by other people around you without you saying them out loud?\n\nPatient: I don’t think they’re, like, putting thoughts in my head exactly. Or hearing them word-for-word. It’s more… they already know what they’re looking for with me.\n\nLike at work, with the cameras on the unit, I’m sure the supervisors are watching me to see if I’m unsafe. Then my shifts get changed, and everyone keeps saying it’s staffing shortages, but that doesn’t make sense to me because it happens after they’ve been watching. So maybe they don’t hear my thoughts, but they know enough. They act like they know what I’m going to do before I do it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about it more, and I probably look nervous, and then that probably just makes them watch more. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being placed in your mind by someone else, or like they're being removed from your mind, or that other people can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: I mean… not exactly like somebody is taking my thoughts out or putting them in, no. I don’t know how that would even feel.\n\nBut people act like they already know something about me. Like at work, the supervisors watch through the unit cameras, and then suddenly my shifts get changed. Everybody keeps saying it’s just staffing shortages, like I’m supposed to just accept that, but it keeps happening after they’ve been watching me. So it feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nIt’s not that they can hear every thought in my head. It’s more like they know enough from watching, and then they do things because of it. And then I start thinking about that all day, even when I’m trying to just do my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain actions or events that don’t seem to involve you are actually about you, like people are talking about you when you’re not around or making decisions based on something you did, even if others don’t see that connection?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that happens. Like with my shifts at work. People act like it’s just scheduling or staffing shortages, but I don’t buy that.\n\nThey’ve got the unit cameras, and I’m sure the supervisors are watching me on them, checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong. Then after that, my shifts get changed. And everybody says, “No, it’s not about you,” but it feels very clearly about me.\n\nI mean, I’ll be trying to just do my rounds or whatever, and then I notice little things, like who’s standing at the desk, who goes quiet when I walk by—well, I guess that’s not really the question. But the shift thing, yeah. That doesn’t feel random. It feels like a decision they make because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that happen around you — like people’s actions, TV shows, or random events — are secretly about you or meant to send you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not like TV shows or, I don’t know, billboards or something. I don’t really mean it like that.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like the cameras on the unit — I’m sure the supervisors watch me through them, and then my shifts get changed. People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, but it always feels like it happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess something up.\n\nAnd then everyone acts normal, like I’m supposed to not notice. I used to try to ignore it and just do my job, but once you see the pattern it’s hard to unsee it. Like even if they don’t say it directly, it feels like the whole thing is about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like something outside of yourself is directly controlling your actions or thoughts, like someone or something is making you think or act in ways you don’t want to?\n\nPatient: Not like… someone is moving my arms or making words come out of my mouth. I don’t think it’s like that.\n\nBut at work, it kind of feels like they’re controlling what happens to me, if that makes sense. Like the supervisors watch me through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get changed. Everybody keeps saying it’s just staffing shortages, but that’s too easy. It’s always after they’ve been watching, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nAnd then I start changing how I act because of it, like trying not to look nervous, trying to be careful where I stand, and then it makes me look worse probably. I don’t know. So not controlling my thoughts exactly, but they get in my head, because I know they’re watching.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… I mean, mostly at work. Like people will say, “Oh, it’s just staffing,” or “Your shift got moved because someone called out,” but it doesn’t feel random to me.

People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, like that explains everything, but it doesn’t. They move me around and watch how I act, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe or something.\n\nAnd then if I try to explain that, it sounds like I’m not answering right, but that’s the thing I mean. It’s not my thoughts being pulled out, it’s them already checking on me and changing things because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that are happening around you, like things people say or do, or events in general, are directly related to you in a special way that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s more like what I mean. Like, at work, when the supervisors move my shifts around, it’s not random to me. They say it’s staffing shortages, everybody keeps saying that, but it feels too lined up with what I’m doing.\n\nLike the cameras on the unit are there, and then I’ll notice someone watching me, or acting different, and then suddenly my schedule changes or I’m put somewhere else. It feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe. Other people don’t seem to see that connection, but I do.\n\nAnd I know it sounds like I’m making a big thing out of scheduling, but it’s not just scheduling. It’s the way it happens after they’ve been watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random things people do or say—like something on TV, a song playing, or even strangers talking—are sending you secret messages meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Not like songs or TV so much… I don’t really watch TV the same way lately anyway, it’s usually just on in the room and people are talking over it.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like I said, the supervisors… they watch through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get moved around. People keep saying, “Oh, it’s staffing,” but it’s not just that. It happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong.\n\nSo I don’t know if that counts as secret messages. It’s not a song telling me something. It’s more like the things they do are about me, and everybody acts like I’m not supposed to notice.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of yourself, like someone or something is influencing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if controlled is the right word. Not like a remote control or something. It’s more like… when I’m at work, I start thinking about how I’m acting because I know they’re watching through the unit cameras.\n\nAnd then the supervisors change my shifts, and everybody says, “It’s just staffing shortages,” but it doesn’t feel like just staffing. It’s always after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe.\n\nSo maybe that influences my thoughts, yeah, because I can’t stop thinking about what they’re seeing or what they’re going to do with it. But I wouldn’t say they’re putting thoughts in there. It’s just… they’re watching, and then things change because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like the thoughts you’re having are being placed in your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being heard or known by other people around you without you saying them out loud?\n\nPatient: I don’t think they’re, like, putting thoughts in my head exactly. Or hearing them word-for-word. It’s more… they already know what they’re looking for with me.\n\nLike at work, with the cameras on the unit, I’m sure the supervisors are watching me to see if I’m unsafe. Then my shifts get changed, and everyone keeps saying it’s staffing shortages, but that doesn’t make sense to me because it happens after they’ve been watching. So maybe they don’t hear my thoughts, but they know enough. They act like they know what I’m going to do before I do it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about it more, and I probably look nervous, and then that probably just makes them watch more. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being placed in your mind by someone else, or like they're being removed from your mind, or that other people can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: I mean… not exactly like somebody is taking my thoughts out or putting them in, no. I don’t know how that would even feel.\n\nBut people act like they already know something about me. Like at work, the supervisors watch through the unit cameras, and then suddenly my shifts get changed. Everybody keeps saying it’s just staffing shortages, like I’m supposed to just accept that, but it keeps happening after they’ve been watching me. So it feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nIt’s not that they can hear every thought in my head. It’s more like they know enough from watching, and then they do things because of it. And then I start thinking about that all day, even when I’m trying to just do my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain actions or events that don’t seem to involve you are actually about you, like people are talking about you when you’re not around or making decisions based on something you did, even if others don’t see that connection?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that happens. Like with my shifts at work. People act like it’s just scheduling or staffing shortages, but I don’t buy that.\n\nThey’ve got the unit cameras, and I’m sure the supervisors are watching me on them, checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong. Then after that, my shifts get changed. And everybody says, “No, it’s not about you,” but it feels very clearly about me.\n\nI mean, I’ll be trying to just do my rounds or whatever, and then I notice little things, like who’s standing at the desk, who goes quiet when I walk by—well, I guess that’s not really the question. But the shift thing, yeah. That doesn’t feel random. It feels like a decision they make because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that happen around you — like people’s actions, TV shows, or random events — are secretly about you or meant to send you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not like TV shows or, I don’t know, billboards or something. I don’t really mean it like that.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like the cameras on the unit — I’m sure the supervisors watch me through them, and then my shifts get changed. People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, but it always feels like it happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess something up.\n\nAnd then everyone acts normal, like I’m supposed to not notice. I used to try to ignore it and just do my job, but once you see the pattern it’s hard to unsee it. Like even if they don’t say it directly, it feels like the whole thing is about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like something outside of yourself is directly controlling your actions or thoughts, like someone or something is making you think or act in ways you don’t want to?\n\nPatient: Not like… someone is moving my arms or making words come out of my mouth. I don’t think it’s like that.\n\nBut at work, it kind of feels like they’re controlling what happens to me, if that makes sense. Like the supervisors watch me through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get changed. Everybody keeps saying it’s just staffing shortages, but that’s too easy. It’s always after they’ve been watching, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nAnd then I start changing how I act because of it, like trying not to look nervous, trying to be careful where I stand, and then it makes me look worse probably. I don’t know. So not controlling my thoughts exactly, but they get in my head, because I know they’re watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain everyday events or actions by others — things that might seem normal or random to most people — are actually directly connected to you or have a special meaning for you that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, mostly at work. Like people will say, “Oh, it’s just staffing,” or “Your shift got moved because someone called out,” but it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nIt feels connected to me because the supervisors have the unit cameras, and I’m sure they’re watching me through them, like checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up. And then suddenly my shifts change. That’s not just nothing.\n\nI know other people don’t see it that way. Like they made that decision because of me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Not TV or songs, not really. I mean, sometimes people bring up stuff like that and it sounds… I don’t know, too dramatic.

For me it’s more the stuff at work. Like the supervisors with the unit cameras. Like I’ll start trying to explain something from an overnight shift, and then somehow I’m talking about soccer, or paint colors, or patient charts, and people just kind of stare at me like they’re lost. I can see it happening, but I can’t seem to stop the jumping around.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any unusual thoughts or perceptions, like hearing voices or seeing things that others don’t?\n\nPatient: Not really like… voices, I don’t think. It’s more that I know something is going on at work. The supervisors keep changing my shifts, and everyone says, “Oh, it’s just staffing shortages,” but that doesn’t feel right. It’s like they’re watching me through the unit cameras, checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nAnd I mean, cameras are there anyway, right? I try to explain it and then they just say I’m stressed, but I’m pretty sure they’re monitoring me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain events, actions, or even things people say are specifically directed at you or have a special meaning for you that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s kind of what I mean. Like with the shift changes, it’s not just random. People keep saying it’s staffing shortages, but I’m sure the supervisors changed my shifts on purpose, like they’re testing me or watching if I’m unsafe.\n\nAnd the cameras on the unit… everyone acts like, “Well, of course there are cameras,” but it’s the way they look at me after, you know? Like something happened on the footage, or they’re checking me. I’ll be trying to explain that, and then I think about how the night shift used to be easier because the halls were quieter, except then they changed that too, and it all kind of points back to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being taken out of your head or broadcasted to others?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about thoughts being put in or taken out exactly… it’s not like I can point to that. It’s more like they already know things, like the supervisors are watching me through the unit cameras and then changing my shifts after, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe.\n\nPeople keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, but that doesn’t line up to me. Because the cameras are there, and then someone looks at me a certain way, and then my schedule gets moved, and then I’m on a different floor or different hours, and… sorry, I know that’s not exactly what you asked. But it feels connected. Like they’re monitoring me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being placed in your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being removed from your mind, or even that your thoughts are being heard by others around you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s like… somebody putting thoughts in there, no. Or taking them out. I don’t really know what that would feel like.\n\nThe heard-by-others thing… I mean, sometimes I wonder if people know what I’m thinking, but then I don’t know if that’s just because I’m acting different and they can tell. Like if I’m quiet on the unit, people notice, and then they start looking at me, and then I start thinking about what I was doing before that, and it kind of… I lose the thread.\n\nSo I guess, no, not exactly like my thoughts are being broadcast. I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being placed in your mind by someone else, or like they're being taken out of your head, or that other people can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Not really like someone is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out. I keep saying that part isn’t… it’s not exactly it.\n\nIt’s more that I know they’re watching me. Like the supervisors, through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get changed after. People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, like that explains everything, but it doesn’t. They move me around and watch how I act, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe or something.\n\nAnd then if I try to explain that, it sounds like I’m not answering right, but that’s the thing I mean. It’s not my thoughts being pulled out, it’s them already checking on me and changing things because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that are happening around you, like things people say or do, or events in general, are directly related to you in a special way that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s more like what I mean. Like, at work, when the supervisors move my shifts around, it’s not random to me. They say it’s staffing shortages, everybody keeps saying that, but it feels too lined up with what I’m doing.\n\nLike the cameras on the unit are there, and then I’ll notice someone watching me, or acting different, and then suddenly my schedule changes or I’m put somewhere else. It feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe. Other people don’t seem to see that connection, but I do.\n\nAnd I know it sounds like I’m making a big thing out of scheduling, but it’s not just scheduling. It’s the way it happens after they’ve been watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random things people do or say—like something on TV, a song playing, or even strangers talking—are sending you secret messages meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Not like songs or TV so much… I don’t really watch TV the same way lately anyway, it’s usually just on in the room and people are talking over it.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like I said, the supervisors… they watch through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get moved around. People keep saying, “Oh, it’s staffing,” but it’s not just that. It happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong.\n\nSo I don’t know if that counts as secret messages. It’s not a song telling me something. It’s more like the things they do are about me, and everybody acts like I’m not supposed to notice.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of yourself, like someone or something is influencing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if controlled is the right word. Not like a remote control or something. It’s more like… when I’m at work, I start thinking about how I’m acting because I know they’re watching through the unit cameras.\n\nAnd then the supervisors change my shifts, and everybody says, “It’s just staffing shortages,” but it doesn’t feel like just staffing. It’s always after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe.\n\nSo maybe that influences my thoughts, yeah, because I can’t stop thinking about what they’re seeing or what they’re going to do with it. But I wouldn’t say they’re putting thoughts in there. It’s just… they’re watching, and then things change because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like the thoughts you’re having are being placed in your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being heard or known by other people around you without you saying them out loud?\n\nPatient: I don’t think they’re, like, putting thoughts in my head exactly. Or hearing them word-for-word. It’s more… they already know what they’re looking for with me.\n\nLike at work, with the cameras on the unit, I’m sure the supervisors are watching me to see if I’m unsafe. Then my shifts get changed, and everyone keeps saying it’s staffing shortages, but that doesn’t make sense to me because it happens after they’ve been watching. So maybe they don’t hear my thoughts, but they know enough. They act like they know what I’m going to do before I do it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about it more, and I probably look nervous, and then that probably just makes them watch more. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being placed in your mind by someone else, or like they're being removed from your mind, or that other people can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: I mean… not exactly like somebody is taking my thoughts out or putting them in, no. I don’t know how that would even feel.\n\nBut people act like they already know something about me. Like at work, the supervisors watch through the unit cameras, and then suddenly my shifts get changed. Everybody keeps saying it’s just staffing shortages, like I’m supposed to just accept that, but it keeps happening after they’ve been watching me. So it feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nIt’s not that they can hear every thought in my head. It’s more like they know enough from watching, and then they do things because of it. And then I start thinking about that all day, even when I’m trying to just do my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain actions or events that don’t seem to involve you are actually about you, like people are talking about you when you’re not around or making decisions based on something you did, even if others don’t see that connection?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that happens. Like with my shifts at work. People act like it’s just scheduling or staffing shortages, but I don’t buy that.\n\nThey’ve got the unit cameras, and I’m sure the supervisors are watching me on them, checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong. Then after that, my shifts get changed. But the shift thing, yeah. That doesn’t feel random. It feels like a decision they make because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that happen around you — like people’s actions, TV shows, or random events — are secretly about you or meant to send you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not like TV shows or, I don’t know, billboards or something. Other people don’t seem to see that connection, but I do.\n\nAnd I know it sounds like I’m making a big thing out of scheduling, but it’s not just scheduling. It’s the way it happens after they’ve been watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random things people do or say—like something on TV, a song playing, or even strangers talking—are sending you secret messages meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Not like songs or TV so much… I don’t really watch TV the same way lately anyway, it’s usually just on in the room and people are talking over it.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like I said, the supervisors… they watch through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get moved around. People keep saying, “Oh, it’s staffing,” but it’s not just that. It happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong.\n\nSo I don’t know if that counts as secret messages. It’s not a song telling me something. It’s more like the things they do are about me, and everybody acts like I’m not supposed to notice.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of yourself, like someone or something is influencing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if controlled is the right word. Not like a remote control or something. It’s more like… when I’m at work, I start thinking about how I’m acting because I know they’re watching through the unit cameras.\n\nAnd then the supervisors change my shifts, and everybody says, “It’s just staffing shortages,” but it doesn’t feel like just staffing. It’s always after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe.\n\nSo maybe that influences my thoughts, yeah, because I can’t stop thinking about what they’re seeing or what they’re going to do with it. But I wouldn’t say they’re putting thoughts in there. It’s just… they’re watching, and then things change because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like the thoughts you’re having are being placed in your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being heard or known by other people around you without you saying them out loud?\n\nPatient: I don’t think they’re, like, putting thoughts in my head exactly. Or hearing them word-for-word. It’s more… they already know what they’re looking for with me.\n\nLike at work, with the cameras on the unit, I’m sure the supervisors are watching me to see if I’m unsafe. Then my shifts get changed, and everyone keeps saying it’s staffing shortages, but that doesn’t make sense to me because it happens after they’ve been watching. So maybe they don’t hear my thoughts, but they know enough. They act like they know what I’m going to do before I do it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about it more, and I probably look nervous, and then that probably just makes them watch more. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being placed in your mind by someone else, or like they're being removed from your mind, or that other people can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: I mean… not exactly like somebody is taking my thoughts out or putting them in, no. I don’t know how that would even feel.\n\nBut people act like they already know something about me. Like at work, the supervisors watch through the unit cameras, and then suddenly my shifts get changed. Everybody keeps saying it’s just staffing shortages, like I’m supposed to just accept that, but it keeps happening after they’ve been watching me. So it feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nIt’s not that they can hear every thought in my head. It’s more like they know enough from watching, and then they do things because of it. And then I start thinking about that all day, even when I’m trying to just do my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain actions or events that don’t seem to involve you are actually about you, like people are talking about you when you’re not around or making decisions based on something you did, even if others don’t see that connection?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that happens. Like with my shifts at work. People act like it’s just scheduling or staffing shortages, but I don’t buy that.\n\nThey’ve got the unit cameras, and I’m sure the supervisors are watching me on them, checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong. Then after that, my shifts get changed. And everybody says, “No, it’s not about you,” but it feels very clearly about me.\n\nI mean, I’ll be trying to just do my rounds or whatever, and then I notice little things, like who’s standing at the desk, who goes quiet when I walk by—well, I guess that’s not really the question. But the shift thing, yeah. That doesn’t feel random. It feels like a decision they make because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that happen around you — like people’s actions, TV shows, or random events — are secretly about you or meant to send you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not like TV shows or, I don’t know, billboards or something. I don’t really mean it like that.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like the cameras on the unit — I’m sure the supervisors watch me through them, and then my shifts get changed. People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, but it always feels like it happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess something up.\n\nAnd then everyone acts normal, like I’m supposed to not notice. I used to try to ignore it and just do my job, but once you see the pattern it’s hard to unsee it. Like even if they don’t say it directly, it feels like the whole thing is about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like something outside of yourself is directly controlling your actions or thoughts, like someone or something is making you think or act in ways you don’t want to?\n\nPatient: Not like… someone is moving my arms or making words come out of my mouth. I don’t think it’s like that.\n\nBut at work, it kind of feels like they’re controlling what happens to me, if that makes sense. Like the supervisors watch me through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get changed. Everybody keeps saying it’s just staffing shortages, but that’s too easy. It’s always after they’ve been watching, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nAnd then I start changing how I act because of it, like trying not to look nervous, trying to be careful where I stand, and then it makes me look worse probably. I don’t know. So not controlling my thoughts exactly, but they get in my head, because I know they’re watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain everyday events or actions by others — things that might seem normal or random to most people — are actually directly connected to you or have a special meaning for you that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, mostly at work. Like people will say, “Oh, it’s just staffing,” or “Your shift got moved because someone called out,” but it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nIt feels connected to me because the supervisors have the unit cameras, and I’m sure they’re watching me through them, like checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.
