I keep saying that part isn’t… it’s not exactly it.\n\nIt’s more that I know they’re watching me. Like the supervisors, through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get changed after. People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, like that explains everything, but it doesn’t. They move me around and watch how I act, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe or something.\n\nAnd then if I try to explain that, it sounds like I’m not answering right, but that’s the thing I mean. It’s not my thoughts being pulled out, it’s them already checking on me and changing things because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that are happening around you, like things people say or do, or events in general, are directly related to you in a special way that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s more like what I mean. Like, at work, when the supervisors move my shifts around, it’s not random to me. They say it’s staffing shortages, everybody keeps saying that, but it feels too lined up with what I’m doing.\n\nLike the cameras on the unit are there, and then I’ll notice someone watching me, or acting different, and then suddenly my schedule changes or I’m put somewhere else. It feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe. Other people don’t seem to see that connection, but I do.\n\nAnd I know it sounds like I’m making a big thing out of scheduling, but it’s not just scheduling. It’s the way it happens after they’ve been watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random things people do or say—like something on TV, a song playing, or even strangers talking—are sending you secret messages meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Not like songs or TV so much… I don’t really watch TV the same way lately anyway, it’s usually just on in the room and people are talking over it.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like I said, the supervisors… they watch through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get moved around. People keep saying, “Oh, it’s staffing,” but it’s not just that. It happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong.\n\nSo I don’t know if that counts as secret messages. It’s not a song telling me something. It’s more like the things they do are about me, and everybody acts like I’m not supposed to notice.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of yourself, like someone or something is influencing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if controlled is the right word. Not like a remote control or something. It’s more like… when I’m at work, I start thinking about how I’m acting because I know they’re watching through the unit cameras.\n\nAnd then the supervisors change my shifts, and everybody says, “It’s just staffing shortages,” but it doesn’t feel like just staffing. It’s always after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe.\n\nSo maybe that influences my thoughts, yeah, because I can’t stop thinking about what they’re seeing or what they’re going to do with it. But I wouldn’t say they’re putting thoughts in there. It’s just… they’re watching, and then things change because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like the thoughts you’re having are being placed in your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being heard or known by other people around you without you saying them out loud?\n\nPatient: I don’t think they’re, like, putting thoughts in my head exactly. Or hearing them word-for-word. It’s more… they already know what they’re looking for with me.\n\nLike at work, with the cameras on the unit, I’m sure the supervisors are watching me to see if I’m unsafe. Then my shifts get changed, and everyone keeps saying it’s staffing shortages, but that doesn’t make sense to me because it happens after they’ve been watching. So maybe they don’t hear my thoughts, but they know enough. They act like they know what I’m going to do before I do it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about it more, and I probably look nervous, and then that probably just makes them watch more. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being placed in your mind by someone else, or like they're being removed from your mind, or that other people can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: I mean… not exactly like somebody is taking my thoughts out or putting them in, no. I don’t know how that would even feel.\n\nBut people act like they already know something about me. Like at work, the supervisors watch through the unit cameras, and then suddenly my shifts get changed. Everybody keeps saying it’s just staffing shortages, like I’m supposed to just accept that, but it keeps happening after they’ve been watching me. So it feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nIt’s not that they can hear every thought in my head. It’s more like they know enough from watching, and then they do things because of it. And then I start thinking about that all day, even when I’m trying to just do my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain actions or events that don’t seem to involve you are actually about you, like people are talking about you when you’re not around or making decisions based on something you did, even if others don’t see that connection?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that happens. Like with my shifts at work. People act like it’s just scheduling or staffing shortages, but I don’t buy that.\n\nThey’ve got the unit cameras, and I’m sure the supervisors are watching me on them, checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong. Then after that, my shifts get changed. And everybody says, “No, it’s not about you,” but it feels very clearly about me.\n\nI mean, I’ll be trying to just do my rounds or whatever, and then I notice little things, like who’s standing at the desk, who goes quiet when I walk by—well, I guess that’s not really the question. But the shift thing, yeah. That doesn’t feel random. It feels like a decision they make because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that happen around you — like people’s actions, TV shows, or random events — are secretly about you or meant to send you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not like TV shows or, I don’t know, billboards or something. I don’t really mean it like that.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like the cameras on the unit — I’m sure the supervisors watch me through them, and then my shifts get changed. People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, but it always feels like it happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess something up.\n\nAnd then everyone acts normal, like I’m supposed to not notice. I used to try to ignore it and just do my job, but once you see the pattern it’s hard to unsee it. Like even if they don’t say it directly, it feels like the whole thing is about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like something outside of yourself is directly controlling your actions or thoughts, like someone or something is making you think or act in ways you don’t want to?\n\nPatient: Not like… someone is moving my arms or making words come out of my mouth. I don’t think it’s like that.\n\nBut at work, it kind of feels like they’re controlling what happens to me, if that makes sense. Like the supervisors watch me through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get changed. Everybody keeps saying it’s just staffing shortages, but that’s too easy. It’s always after they’ve been watching, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nAnd then I start changing how I act because of it, like trying not to look nervous, trying to be careful where I stand, and then it makes me look worse probably. I don’t know. So not controlling my thoughts exactly, but they get in my head, because I know they’re watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain everyday events or actions by others — things that might seem normal or random to most people — are actually directly connected to you or have a special meaning for you that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, mostly at work. Like people will say, “Oh, it’s just staffing,” or “Your shift got moved because someone called out,” but it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nIt feels connected to me because the supervisors have the unit cameras, and I’m sure they’re watching me through them, like checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up. And then suddenly my shifts change. That’s not just nothing.\n\nI know other people don’t see it that way. They act like I’m reading too much into it, but… I don’t know, when it keeps happening like that, it feels like it means something. Like they made that decision because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people do or say—like something on TV, a song playing, or even strangers talking—are sending you secret messages meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Not TV or songs, not really. I mean, sometimes people bring up stuff like that and it sounds… I don’t know, too dramatic.\n\nFor me it’s more the stuff at work. Like the supervisors with the unit cameras. I’m sure they watch me through those, and then my shifts get changed, and everyone says, “No, it’s staffing shortages,” like that explains it. But it happens after they’ve been watching, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess something up.\n\nAnd then people talk around it like it’s normal, and I’m supposed to just accept that. But it feels like it’s meant for me, like the schedule change is because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random things people do or say—like something on TV, a song playing, or even strangers talking—are sending you secret messages meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Not like songs or TV talking to me, no. It’s more… like at work, the things people say are supposed to sound normal, like “staffing shortages,” but I don’t buy that.\n\nThe supervisors have those unit cameras, and I’m sure they’re watching me through them. Then my shifts get changed, and everyone acts like it’s just the schedule, like I’m supposed to just shrug and go home or come in at a different time. But it feels like it’s aimed at me, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nI know that probably sounds like I’m taking it too personally, but when it keeps happening, it doesn’t feel random. It feels connected to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I do. I know people say it’s just normal work stuff, like staffing shortages or somebody called out, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.

Like the supervisors have those unit cameras, and I’m sure they watch me through them. Not just casually, like actually watching to see if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess something up. And then my shifts get changed after that, and everyone acts like it’s just scheduling.

It reminds me of when people say, “Oh, don’t take things personally,” but how am I not supposed to when it keeps happening around me? I worked hard to get that job, and I try to keep my head down, but then it’s like they’re moving me around for a reason. So yeah… it feels connected to me, even if other people don’t notice it.

I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being placed in your mind by someone else, or like they're being taken out of your head, or that other people can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: Not really like someone is putting thoughts in my head or taking them out. Like, at work, when the supervisors move my shifts around, it’s not random to me. They say it’s staffing shortages, everybody keeps saying that, but it feels too lined up with what I’m doing.\n\nLike the cameras on the unit are there, and then I’ll notice someone watching me, or acting different, and then suddenly my schedule changes or I’m put somewhere else. It feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe. Other people don’t seem to see that connection, but I do.\n\nAnd I know it sounds like I’m making a big thing out of scheduling, but it’s not just scheduling. It’s the way it happens after they’ve been watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random things people do or say—like something on TV, a song playing, or even strangers talking—are sending you secret messages meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Not like songs or TV so much… I don’t really watch TV the same way lately anyway, it’s usually just on in the room and people are talking over it.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like I said, the supervisors… they watch through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get moved around. People keep saying, “Oh, it’s staffing,” but it’s not just that. It happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong.\n\nSo I don’t know if that counts as secret messages. It’s not a song telling me something. It’s more like the things they do are about me, and everybody acts like I’m not supposed to notice.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being controlled by something outside of yourself, like someone or something is influencing them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if controlled is the right word. Not like a remote control or something. It’s more like… when I’m at work, I start thinking about how I’m acting because I know they’re watching through the unit cameras.\n\nAnd then the supervisors change my shifts, and everybody says, “It’s just staffing shortages,” but it doesn’t feel like just staffing. It’s always after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe.\n\nSo maybe that influences my thoughts, yeah, because I can’t stop thinking about what they’re seeing or what they’re going to do with it. But I wouldn’t say they’re putting thoughts in there. It’s just… they’re watching, and then things change because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like the thoughts you’re having are being placed in your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being heard or known by other people around you without you saying them out loud?\n\nPatient: I don’t think they’re, like, putting thoughts in my head exactly. Or hearing them word-for-word. It’s more… they already know what they’re looking for with me.\n\nLike at work, with the cameras on the unit, I’m sure the supervisors are watching me to see if I’m unsafe. Then my shifts get changed, and everyone keeps saying it’s staffing shortages, but that doesn’t make sense to me because it happens after they’ve been watching. So maybe they don’t hear my thoughts, but they know enough. They act like they know what I’m going to do before I do it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about it more, and I probably look nervous, and then that probably just makes them watch more. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being placed in your mind by someone else, or like they're being removed from your mind, or that other people can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: I mean… not exactly like somebody is taking my thoughts out or putting them in, no. I don’t know how that would even feel.\n\nBut people act like they already know something about me. Like at work, the supervisors watch through the unit cameras, and then suddenly my shifts get changed. Everybody keeps saying it’s just staffing shortages, like I’m supposed to just accept that, but it keeps happening after they’ve been watching me. So it feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nIt’s not that they can hear every thought in my head. It’s more like they know enough from watching, and then they do things because of it. And then I start thinking about that all day, even when I’m trying to just do my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain actions or events that don’t seem to involve you are actually about you, like people are talking about you when you’re not around or making decisions based on something you did, even if others don’t see that connection?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that happens. But the shift thing, yeah. That doesn’t feel random. It feels like a decision they make because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that happen around you — like people’s actions, TV shows, or random events — are secretly about you or meant to send you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not like TV shows or, I don’t know, billboards or something. I don’t really mean it like that.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like the cameras on the unit — I’m sure the supervisors watch me through them, and then my shifts get changed. People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, but it always feels like it happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess something up.\n\nAnd then everyone acts normal, like I’m supposed to not notice. I used to try to ignore it and just do my job, but once you see the pattern it’s hard to unsee it. Like even if they don’t say it directly, it feels like the whole thing is about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like something outside of yourself is directly controlling your actions or thoughts, like someone or something is making you think or act in ways you don’t want to?\n\nPatient: Not like… someone is moving my arms or making words come out of my mouth. I don’t think it’s like that.\n\nBut at work, it kind of feels like they’re controlling what happens to me, if that makes sense. Like the supervisors watch me through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get changed. Everybody keeps saying it’s just staffing shortages, but that’s too easy. I don’t know. They act like I’m reading too much into it, but… I don’t know, when it keeps happening like that, it feels like it means something. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say “someone” like some big thing, but at work it feels like that.

Like the supervisors, with the unit cameras—they can see everything. And I’m sure they’re watching me through those, not just for normal stuff, but to see if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up. Then my shifts get changed, and everybody says, “Oh, it’s staffing shortages,” like that’s supposed to make me feel stupid for noticing.

But it feels controlled, like it’s being done to send me the message that they’re watching me, that they don’t trust me there. I try to just do my job, you know, keep moving, help with the patients, clean up, all that… but then the schedule changes and it’s like, okay, why me again? It doesn’t feel random.

It’s always after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe.\n\nSo maybe that influences my thoughts, yeah, because I can’t stop thinking about what they’re seeing or what they’re going to do with it. But I wouldn’t say they’re putting thoughts in there. It’s just… they’re watching, and then things change because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like the thoughts you’re having are being placed in your mind by someone else, or that your thoughts are being heard or known by other people around you without you saying them out loud?\n\nPatient: I don’t think they’re, like, putting thoughts in my head exactly. Or hearing them word-for-word. It’s more… they already know what they’re looking for with me.\n\nLike at work, with the cameras on the unit, I’m sure the supervisors are watching me to see if I’m unsafe. Then my shifts get changed, and everyone keeps saying it’s staffing shortages, but that doesn’t make sense to me because it happens after they’ve been watching. So maybe they don’t hear my thoughts, but they know enough. They act like they know what I’m going to do before I do it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about it more, and I probably look nervous, and then that probably just makes them watch more. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like your thoughts are being placed in your mind by someone else, or like they're being removed from your mind, or that other people can hear your thoughts?\n\nPatient: I mean… not exactly like somebody is taking my thoughts out or putting them in, no. I don’t know how that would even feel.\n\nBut people act like they already know something about me. Like at work, the supervisors watch through the unit cameras, and then suddenly my shifts get changed. Everybody keeps saying it’s just staffing shortages, like I’m supposed to just accept that, but it keeps happening after they’ve been watching me. So it feels like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nIt’s not that they can hear every thought in my head. It’s more like they know enough from watching, and then they do things because of it. And then I start thinking about that all day, even when I’m trying to just do my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain actions or events that don’t seem to involve you are actually about you, like people are talking about you when you’re not around or making decisions based on something you did, even if others don’t see that connection?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that happens. Like with my shifts at work. People act like it’s just scheduling or staffing shortages, but I don’t buy that.\n\nThey’ve got the unit cameras, and I’m sure the supervisors are watching me on them, checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to do something wrong. Then after that, my shifts get changed. And everybody says, “No, it’s not about you,” but it feels very clearly about me.\n\nI mean, I’ll be trying to just do my rounds or whatever, and then I notice little things, like who’s standing at the desk, who goes quiet when I walk by—well, I guess that’s not really the question. But the shift thing, yeah. That doesn’t feel random. It feels like a decision they make because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that happen around you — like people’s actions, TV shows, or random events — are secretly about you or meant to send you a message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not like TV shows or, I don’t know, billboards or something. I don’t really mean it like that.\n\nIt’s more at work. Like the cameras on the unit — I’m sure the supervisors watch me through them, and then my shifts get changed. People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, but it always feels like it happens after they’ve been watching me, like they’re trying to see if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess something up.\n\nAnd then everyone acts normal, like I’m supposed to not notice. I used to try to ignore it and just do my job, but once you see the pattern it’s hard to unsee it. Like even if they don’t say it directly, it feels like the whole thing is about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like something outside of yourself is directly controlling your actions or thoughts, like someone or something is making you think or act in ways you don’t want to?\n\nPatient: Not like… someone is moving my arms or making words come out of my mouth. I don’t think it’s like that.\n\nBut at work, it kind of feels like they’re controlling what happens to me, if that makes sense. Like the supervisors watch me through the unit cameras, and then my shifts get changed. Everybody keeps saying it’s just staffing shortages, but that’s too easy. It’s always after they’ve been watching, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nAnd then I start changing how I act because of it, like trying not to look nervous, trying to be careful where I stand, and then it makes me look worse probably. I don’t know. So not controlling my thoughts exactly, but they get in my head, because I know they’re watching.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain everyday events or actions by others — things that might seem normal or random to most people — are actually directly connected to you or have a special meaning for you that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, mostly at work. Like people will say, “Oh, it’s just staffing,” or “Your shift got moved because someone called out,” but it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nIt feels connected to me because the supervisors have the unit cameras, and I’m sure they’re watching me through them, like checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up. And then suddenly my shifts change. That’s not just nothing.\n\nI know other people don’t see it that way. They act like I’m reading too much into it, but… I don’t know, when it keeps happening like that, it feels like it means something. Like they made that decision because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things people do or say—like something on TV, a song playing, or even strangers talking—are sending you secret messages meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Not TV or songs, not really. I mean, sometimes people bring up stuff like that and it sounds… I don’t know, too dramatic.\n\nFor me it’s more the stuff at work. Like the supervisors with the unit cameras. I’m sure they watch me through those, and then my shifts get changed, and everyone says, “No, it’s staffing shortages,” like that explains it. But it happens after they’ve been watching, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess something up.\n\nAnd then people talk around it like it’s normal, and I’m supposed to just accept that. But it feels like it’s meant for me, like the schedule change is because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like random things people do or say—like something on TV, a song playing, or even strangers talking—are sending you secret messages meant just for you?\n\nPatient: Not like songs or TV talking to me, no. It’s more… like at work, the things people say are supposed to sound normal, like “staffing shortages,” but I don’t buy that.\n\nThe supervisors have those unit cameras, and I’m sure they’re watching me through them. Then my shifts get changed, and everyone acts like it’s just the schedule, like I’m supposed to just shrug and go home or come in at a different time. But it feels like it’s aimed at me, like they’re checking if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up.\n\nI know that probably sounds like I’m taking it too personally, but when it keeps happening, it doesn’t feel random. It feels connected to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that certain everyday events or actions by others—things that might seem normal or random to most people—are actually directly connected to you or have a special meaning for you that others don’t seem to notice?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I know people say it’s just normal work stuff, like staffing shortages or somebody called out, but it doesn’t feel like that to me.\n\nLike the supervisors have those unit cameras, and I’m sure they watch me through them. Not just casually, like actually watching to see if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess something up. And then my shifts get changed after that, and everyone acts like it’s just scheduling.\n\nIt reminds me of when people say, “Oh, don’t take things personally,” but how am I not supposed to when it keeps happening around me? I worked hard to get that job, and I try to keep my head down, but then it’s like they’re moving me around for a reason. So yeah… it feels connected to me, even if other people don’t notice it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things that are happening around you, like people’s actions or events, are directly controlled by someone or something to send you a specific message?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d say “someone” like some big thing, but at work it feels like that.\n\nLike the supervisors, with the unit cameras—they can see everything. And I’m sure they’re watching me through those, not just for normal stuff, but to see if I’m unsafe or if I’m going to mess up. Then my shifts get changed, and everybody says, “Oh, it’s staffing shortages,” like that’s supposed to make me feel stupid for noticing.\n\nBut it feels controlled, like it’s being done to send me the message that they’re watching me, that they don’t trust me there. I try to just do my job, you know, keep moving, help with the patients, clean up, all that… but then the schedule changes and it’s like, okay, why me again? It doesn’t feel random."
